# 2.0 Configure Duty Rules

*Creates and maintains the Duty Rule rows that express how duties rank candidates. Derived from the System Design Specification §9. Where this document conflicts with the specification, the specification wins.*

---

## Responsibility

2.0 Configure Duty Rules creates, edits, and deactivates Duty Rule rows. A Duty Rule expresses a single criterion, at a single tier, for a single Duty.

This is the process through which an administrator expresses every ranking and filtering relationship between a Duty and whatever criteria the system supports. It is the only mechanism that connects a Role — or Gender, Age, Tenure, or any other criteria type — to a Duty.

2.0 writes rules. It does not evaluate them. Evaluation belongs to 5.0 Generate Assignment.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.10.

---

## Trigger

Administrator action.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.2.

---

## The footprint

| Direction | Store |
| --- | --- |
| Reads | D1 (Duty, for selection); D2 (existing DutyRules, for display and edit) |
| Writes | D2 |

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.2.

---

## Rule shape

A Duty Rule is a row with:

| Column | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| RuleID | Primary key |
| DutyID | Foreign key → Duty. The duty this rule ranks candidates for. |
| TierOrder | Administrator-defined sequence position. |
| CriteriaType | One of the supported criteria types. |
| CriteriaValue | The administrator-entered value for that criterion. |
| IsActive | True at creation. Deactivated, never deleted. |

**Source:** System Design Specification §4 (Duty Rule), §9.1.10.

---

## The criteria vocabulary

The CriteriaType field is constrained to the fixed vocabulary defined in §5 of the specification:

- Role
- Gender
- Age Range
- Tenure (duration since joining)
- Membership Stage
- Branch-Attendance Recency
- Eligibility Flag

Plus three reserved types, which may be written but are not evaluated:

- Acceptance Rate
- Duties Carried
- Days Since Last Assignment

These are supported criterion *types*, not rules. Their presence in the vocabulary does not activate them anywhere. Which criteria apply to a given Duty is entirely a matter of administrator configuration.

**Source:** System Design Specification §5.

---

## Tier composition

Multiple rules may share the same TierOrder for a single Duty. Rows sharing a TierOrder are ANDed — all of them must be satisfied for a member to match that tier.

No OR operator is provided within a tier. Tiers themselves express ordered-preference OR: the engine tries Tier 1, and only if it produces no candidate does it fall through to Tier 2.

No NOT operator is provided. It is a non-breaking addition to make later if a genuine need appears.

**Source:** System Design Specification §5, §9.1.10.

---

## Tier ordering

TierOrder is an administrator-defined sequence position. Its values are integers. Gaps are permitted — an administrator may leave Tier 2 empty and use Tier 3.

TierOrder does not imply any behavior beyond its position in the ascending sequence.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.10.

---

## CriteriaValue

CriteriaValue is a free-text field in storage. Its interpretation depends on CriteriaType:

- For CriteriaType = Role: the value names a Role.
- For CriteriaType = Age Range: the value is a range (e.g., "13-35").
- For CriteriaType = Gender: the value names a gender.
- And so on.

The system does not validate CriteriaValue at write time. Validation belongs to whichever process evaluates the rule (5.0). A rule with a CriteriaValue that does not match any configured entity simply never matches a member.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.10.

---

## Editing and deactivation

A Duty Rule is editable. Changes to TierOrder, CriteriaType, or CriteriaValue take effect on the next 5.0 run. Existing Roster Assignment rows are not rewritten — the no-backward-propagation rule applies.

A Duty Rule is deactivated, never deleted. A deactivated rule is invisible to 5.0's evaluation.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.1, §9.1.10.

---

## Invariants

- A Duty Rule references an existing Duty.
- Multiple rules may share TierOrder for a single Duty. Same-tier rules are ANDed.
- TierOrder gaps are permitted.
- A deactivated rule is invisible to 5.0.
- Editing a rule does not rewrite any operational record.
- 2.0 never evaluates a rule; it only stores one.
- The CriteriaType vocabulary is fixed at write time. An administrator cannot invent a new criteria type without a specification revision.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.3.

---

## What 2.0 does not do

- It does not evaluate any rule.
- It does not link Role to Duty. A Duty Rule describes a preference; the system reads it as data.
- It does not create Duties. That is 1.0.
- It does not create Eligibility records. That is 4.0.
- It does not invoke any other process.

**Source:** System Design Specification §2, §9.3.

---

## Example shape

Three Duty Rules for a single Duty, expressing "Tier 1: Role = Messenger":

| RuleID | DutyID | TierOrder | CriteriaType | CriteriaValue |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 5 | 1 | Role | Messenger |

A Duty with no rules configured falls into the "no rules" case: every eligible member enters as an equal candidate.

A Duty with two tiers, where Tier 2 is "any eligible member," is expressed as a single rule at Tier 2 with CriteriaType that always matches, or as no further tiers (the engine falls through and every remaining eligible member is a candidate).

**Source:** System Design Specification §5, §9.1.10.

---

## Cross-references

- **Specification §5** — the configuration language and criteria vocabulary.
- **Specification §9** — the full derivation.
- **`1.0-configure-vocabulary.md`** — produces the Role and Duty vocabulary 2.0 references.
- **`4.0-manage-eligibility.md`** — the independent candidacy mechanism.
- **`../operations/5.0-generate-assignment.md`** — the process that evaluates Duty Rules.

---

*Source: System Design Specification §5, §9.1.10, §9.3.*

LINES: 185

# 3.0 Manage Membership

*Creates and maintains Member records and Identifier History. Derived from the System Design Specification §9. Where this document conflicts with the specification, the specification wins.*

---

## Responsibility

3.0 Manage Membership maintains the member register and the identifier history. It creates and edits Member records, and issues, retires, and reassigns identifiers through the Identifier History log.

It does not assign duties (that is 5.0), does not grant eligibility (that is 4.0), and does not evaluate anything.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.7.

---

## Trigger

Administrator action.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.2.

---

## Subprocesses

3.0 decomposes into two subprocesses, matching the DFD's Level 2 decomposition:

| # | Subprocess | Responsibility |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 3.1 | Manage Member Record | Create, edit, deactivate Member records |
| 3.2 | Manage Identifier History | Issue, retire, and reassign member identifiers |

**Source:** Data Flow Diagrams, Level 2 — Manage Membership (3.0).

---

## The 3.0 footprint

| Subprocess | Reads | Writes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 3.1 | D4 (existing Members, for edit and display); D1 (Role); D3 (Branch) | D4 |
| 3.2 | D5 (existing IdentifierHistory); D4 (Member) | D5 |

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.2.

---

## Member record

### Required fields at creation

- JoinDate
- DateOfBirth
- MembershipStage
- Name
- Surname
- Gender
- Phone
- BranchID
- RoleID

Only Email is optional.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.7.

### Cardinality

A Member has exactly one BranchID and exactly one RoleID. There is no MemberRole join table and no MemberBranch join table. A member holds one role and belongs to one branch.

**Source:** System Design Specification §4 (Member).

### MembershipStage

MembershipStage is a free-text field, not a lookup. It is an administrator-defined label stored directly on the Member row.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.7.

### Lifecycle

A Member is created active. Deactivation excludes them from new roster generation without touching any existing operational record. A Member is never deleted.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.1.

### Edits

Changing a Member's BranchID or RoleID affects only future use. Existing operational records (assignments, attendance) retain their historical context, which is anchored to occurrences, not to the member's current attributes.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.7.

---

## Identifier History

### Purpose

Identifier History is a per-member log of identifier assignments. It tracks every identifier a member has held — its type, its number, when it was assigned, and when (if ever) it was retired.

**Source:** System Design Specification §4 (Identifier History).

### Fields

| Column | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| EntryID | Primary key |
| MemberID | Foreign key → Member |
| Type | Free-text administrator-defined category (e.g., "Card", "Receipt") |
| Number | Administrator-supplied |
| AssignedDate | Required |
| UnassignedDate | Optional. Set on retirement. |
| Reason | Optional |
| AuthorizedBy | Required. Foreign key → Admin. |

**Source:** System Design Specification §4 (Identifier History), §9.1.7.

### Lifecycle

Identifier History has no IsActive flag. Entries are added and retired via UnassignedDate; they are never deleted. The entry is a historical record.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.1, §9.1.7.

### Type

Type is a free-text field. It is not a lookup table. The system manages the assignment/history lifecycle without needing to understand what any given category means.

**Source:** System Design Specification §4 (Identifier History), §9.1.7.

### Number

The Number field is administrator-supplied. The system does not generate identifier numbers.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.7.

### Multiple active identifiers

The system does not prevent two active identifiers of the same Type for one member. If an administrator issues two active cards, both rows exist with UnassignedDate = null. This is a configuration error, not a schema error. The system does not enforce uniqueness beyond the EntryID primary key.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.7.

### Reassignment

Reassignment to a different member is permitted if the administrator chooses. The MKN Configuration Reference sets a default of "identifiers are never reused" — a policy, not a system constraint.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.7.

---

## Invariants

- A Member references an existing Branch and Role.
- Identifier History references an existing Member.
- Identifier numbers are administrator-supplied, not system-generated.
- Deactivating a Member does not touch operational records.
- Editing a Member does not rewrite operational records.
- Multiple active identifiers of the same type are permitted by the system.
- 3.0 never invokes any other process.
- 3.0 never reads or writes an operational store.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.3.

---

## What 3.0 does not do

- It does not assign duties.
- It does not grant eligibility.
- It does not create Admin records (that is a separate concern).
- It does not evaluate anything.
- It does not invoke any other process.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.2, §9.3.

---

## Cross-references

- **Specification §9** — the full derivation.
- **`1.0-configure-vocabulary.md`** — produces the Role and Branch vocabulary 3.0 references.
- **`4.0-manage-eligibility.md`** — grants eligibility to members 3.0 has registered.
- **`../operations/5.0-generate-assignment.md`** — reads Member records when evaluating candidates.
- **`../operations/6.0-record-attendance.md`** — reads Member records when recording attendance.

---

*Source: System Design Specification §4, §9.1.7, §9.3.*

# 4.0 Manage Eligibility

*Creates and maintains the Eligibility grant/revoke records that determine duty candidacy. Derived from the System Design Specification §9. Where this document conflicts with the specification, the specification wins.*

---

## Responsibility

4.0 Manage Eligibility creates and manages the binary permission records that determine *candidacy* for a duty. It does not rank candidates — that is 5.2's responsibility — and it does not assign anyone — that is 5.0's responsibility.

Eligibility is the gate. Duty Rule is the ranking. They are independent mechanisms, and 4.0 maintains one of them.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.9.

---

## Trigger

Administrator action.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.2.

---

## The footprint

| Direction | Store |
| --- | --- |
| Reads | D1 (Duty, for selection); D4 (Member, for selection); D6 (existing Eligibility, for display and edit) |
| Writes | D6 |

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.2.

---

## The Eligibility record

An Eligibility row represents a single grant/revoke cycle for a (Member, Duty) pair:

| Column | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| EligibilityID | Primary key — durable identifier |
| MemberID | Foreign key → Member |
| DutyID | Foreign key → Duty |
| GrantedDate | Required |
| GrantedBy | Required. Foreign key → Admin. |
| RevokedDate | Optional. Set on revocation. |
| RevokedReason | Optional |

**Source:** System Design Specification §4 (Eligibility).

---

## Grant and revoke lifecycle

### Grant

A new Eligibility row with GrantedDate set is a grant. The member becomes a candidate for the duty (subject to Duty Rule ranking in 5.0).

### Revoke

Revocation is expressed by setting RevokedDate (and optionally RevokedReason) on an existing row. The row is not deleted.

### Re-grant

A re-grant is a new row. This is why EligibilityID exists as a durable primary key — each grant/revoke cycle is its own row, and the history is preserved.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.9.

---

## Current eligibility resolution

The applicable Eligibility row for a (Member, Duty) pair is determined by:

1. The row whose GrantedDate is in the past.
2. Whose RevokedDate is either null or in the future.
3. If multiple such rows exist, the one with the greatest EligibilityID.

If the resolved row has a RevokedDate in the past, the member is not eligible.

If no row exists at all, the member is not eligible. Eligibility is opt-in — absence is not the same as a grant.

The specific resolution rule is stated in the 5.0 contract, not in the schema. 4.0 does not perform resolution; it stores the records that 5.0 resolves.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.9, §10.

---

## Independence from Duty Rule

Eligibility and Duty Rule are independent. A member can be:

- Eligible for a duty and match every Duty Rule (a strong candidate).
- Eligible for a duty and fail every Duty Rule (falls through to a lower tier or to the no-rules case).
- Ineligible for a duty and match every Duty Rule (never becomes a candidate, because eligibility is the gate).

**Source:** System Design Specification §5, §9.1.9.

---

## Scope

Eligibility is per (Member, Duty). It is not per-branch, per-service, or per-occurrence. If an administrator wants a scoped eligibility, that is expressed through Duty Rule criteria (e.g., ServiceType as a criterion), not through the Eligibility record itself.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.9.

---

## Lifecycle

Eligibility does not have an IsActive flag. Grant/revoke is expressed through GrantedDate and RevokedDate, not through activation state. A revoked record stays in the table as history.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.1, §9.1.9.

---

## Invariants

- Eligibility references an existing Member and an existing Duty.
- A grant creates a new row; a revoke sets RevokedDate on an existing row.
- Current eligibility is determined by the latest applicable grant/revoke record.
- Eligibility is independent of Duty Rule.
- 4.0 does not evaluate anything; it stores permission records.
- Revoking eligibility does not touch existing Roster Assignment rows.
- 4.0 never invokes any other process.
- 4.0 never writes to an operational store.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.3.

---

## What 4.0 does not do

- It does not rank candidates. That is 5.2 (Priority).
- It does not assign members. That is 5.0.
- It does not grant eligibility for all duties at once as a system feature. Each grant is per-duty.
- It does not perform authorization checks. Those are upstream.
- It does not invoke any other process.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.2, §9.3.

---

## Cross-references

- **Specification §5** — the configuration language, including how Eligibility Flag is used as a Duty Rule criterion.
- **Specification §9** — the full derivation.
- **`1.0-configure-vocabulary.md`** — produces the Duty vocabulary 4.0 references.
- **`3.0-manage-membership.md`** — produces the Member vocabulary 4.0 references.
- **`2.0-configure-duty-rules.md`** — the independent ranking mechanism.
- **`../operations/5.0-generate-assignment.md`** — reads Eligibility records as the candidacy gate.

---

*Source: System Design Specification §4, §5, §9.1.9, §9.3.*

# 8.0 Manage Events and Programs

*Creates and maintains Events, Programs, Program Items, and Event Duties. Derived from the System Design Specification §9. Where this document conflicts with the specification, the specification wins.*

---

## Responsibility

8.0 Manage Events and Programs maintains the event lifecycle. It creates and edits Events, their Programs, the Program Items within each Program, and the ad-hoc Event Duties on individual items.

It is the only configuration process permitted to create an operational record — and only because a Program Item has been explicitly linked to a Service Definition.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.11.

---

## Trigger

Administrator action.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.2.

---

## The 8.0 footprint

| Direction | Store |
| --- | --- |
| Reads | D9 (Event, Program, ProgramItem, EventDuty); D3 (ServiceDefinition for ProgramItem.ServiceDefID); D4 (Member for EventDuty.AssignedMemberID); D10 (AssignmentStatus for EventDuty.AssignmentStatusID) |
| Writes | D9; D3 (event-sourced ServiceOccurrence rows only) |

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.2.

---

## The 8.0 boundary

8.0 is the sole configuration process permitted to create an operational record. This exception is deliberate and bounded: it applies only when a Program Item is linked to a Service Definition. In that case, 8.0 creates an event-sourced Service Occurrence.

8.0 does not:

- create schedule-sourced occurrences (that is 11.0)
- run the roster engine
- manage the recurring rostering loop
- send notifications
- evaluate fill status

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.0, §9.3.

---

## Entities maintained

### Event

| Column | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| EventID | Primary key |
| Name |  |
| StartDate |  |
| EndDate |  |
| Location | Free text |
| Type | Free text |
| IsActive | True at creation. Deactivated, never deleted. |

An Event may span multiple days. StartDate and EndDate are both fields.

**Source:** System Design Specification §4 (Event), §9.1.11.

### Program

| Column | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| ProgramID | Primary key |
| EventID | Foreign key → Event. One Program per Event. |
| Title |  |

There is exactly one Program per Event.

**Source:** System Design Specification §4 (Program), §9.1.11.

### ProgramItem

| Column | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| ItemID | Primary key |
| ProgramID | Foreign key → Program |
| SequenceOrder | Required |
| Title |  |
| ScheduledStart | Required. Timestamp. |
| ScheduledEnd | Required. Timestamp. |
| ServiceDefID | Optional. Foreign key → ServiceDefinition. |

**Program Item has no stored status field.** Its live state (upcoming / current / done) is computed at read time from ScheduledStart, ScheduledEnd, and the current time. This is a system-calculated lifecycle, not an administrator-configured one.

**Source:** System Design Specification §4 (Program Item), §9.1.11.

### EventDuty

| Column | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| EventDutyID | Primary key |
| ProgramItemID | Foreign key → ProgramItem |
| Label | Free text, administrator-typed, no preset list |
| AssignedMemberID | Required. Foreign key → Member. Direct pick. |
| AssignmentStatusID | Optional. Foreign key → AssignmentStatus. |

EventDuty is not a reference to the Duty table. It represents an ad-hoc role on an event, not a configurable rostering duty. `Label` is free text; the Assignment is a direct member pick, with no Eligibility or Priority run.

**Source:** System Design Specification §4 (Event Duty), §9.1.11.

---

## Event-sourced occurrence creation

When a Program Item's ServiceDefID is set — at creation or by later edit — the system creates an event-sourced Service Occurrence directly.

The occurrence's values are derived from the **Program Item**, not from a Service Schedule:

| Occurrence column | Derived from |
| --- | --- |
| Date | The date portion of ProgramItem.ScheduledStart |
| StartTime | The time portion of ProgramItem.ScheduledStart |
| ServiceTypeID | The linked ServiceDefinition.ServiceTypeID |
| EventID | The Program Item's Event |
| ScheduleID | null |
| FillStatusID | null |
| GeneratedBy | Administrator |
| CreatedBy | The administrator who created the Program Item |
| ChangedBy | null |

The occurrence's Date and StartTime come from ProgramItem.ScheduledStart. ServiceTypeID is inherited from the linked Service Definition. These are the two separate derivations, and they must not be conflated: the Service Definition supplies the service type, but it does not supply a start time — start time lives on the Program Item in the event case, and on ServiceSchedule in the schedule case.

This occurrence is entirely outside the Occurrence Materializer. It is not subject to 11.0's idempotency, not covered by the (ScheduleID, Date) uniqueness constraint, and not affected by the Materializer's schedule-change or schedule-deactivation rules.

**Source:** System Design Specification §4, §7 (Event-sourced occurrences), §9.1.11.

---

## Comparison with schedule-sourced occurrences

For contrast, a schedule-sourced occurrence (created by 11.0) derives its values from the ServiceSchedule:

| Occurrence column | Schedule-sourced (11.0) | Event-sourced (8.0) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Date | From the materialized date, per ServiceSchedule's TimeSlot.DayOfWeek | Date portion of ProgramItem.ScheduledStart |
| StartTime | From ServiceSchedule.StartTime | Time portion of ProgramItem.ScheduledStart |
| ServiceTypeID | From ServiceSchedule's ServiceDefinition | From the linked ServiceDefinition |
| EventID | null | The Program Item's Event |
| ScheduleID | The ServiceSchedule | null |
| GeneratedBy | System | Administrator |
| CreatedBy | null | The administrator who created the Program Item |

**Source:** System Design Specification §4, §7.

---

## Removal of ServiceDefID

If a Program Item's ServiceDefID is removed after an occurrence was created, the occurrence is not automatically deleted. Event-sourced occurrences are governed by 8.0, and cleanup is an administrator action, not a system behavior.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.11.

---

## Lifecycle

Event, Program, ProgramItem, and EventDuty each carry lifecycle state (except Program, which has no IsActive flag in the current specification — it is one-per-event and deactivated only by deactivating its Event).

Events, ProgramItems, and EventDuties are deactivated, never deleted. Deactivation does not cascade. Existing event-sourced occurrences remain.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.1.1, §9.1.11.

---

## Invariants

- One Program per Event.
- ProgramItem does not store status; it is computed.
- EventDuty references an existing Member directly, with no roster engine run.
- EventDuty.Label is free text, not a Duty reference.
- Event-sourced ServiceOccurrences are created only by 8.0, never by 11.0.
- Event-sourced occurrences have GeneratedBy = Administrator and CreatedBy = the acting admin.
- Event-sourced occurrences derive Date and StartTime from ProgramItem.ScheduledStart.
- Event-sourced occurrences derive ServiceTypeID from the linked ServiceDefinition.
- Deactivation does not cascade and does not touch existing occurrences.
- 8.0 is independent of the recurring roster machinery.
- 8.0 never invokes any other process.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.3.

---

## What 8.0 does not do

- It does not create schedule-sourced occurrences.
- It does not run the roster engine.
- It does not assign members to configurable duties. Event duties are ad-hoc, not duty-table duties.
- It does not affect the recurring roster loop.
- It does not invoke any other process.

**Source:** System Design Specification §9.2, §9.3.

---

## Cross-references

- **Specification §4** — the entity definitions 8.0 creates rows in.
- **Specification §7** — the materialization contract, including the section on event-sourced occurrences.
- **Specification §9** — the full derivation, including the 8.0 exception to the configuration-layer boundary.
- **`1.0-configure-vocabulary.md`** — produces the Service Definition vocabulary 8.0 references.
- **`3.0-manage-membership.md`** — produces the Member vocabulary 8.0 references.

---

*Source: System Design Specification §4, §7, §9.1.11, §9.3.*

# Configuration Layer

*The five processes that create and maintain every administrator-entered value in the system. Derived from the System Design Specification §9. Where this document conflicts with the specification, the specification wins.*

---

## Purpose

The configuration layer is the set of processes through which an administrator enters every organizational choice the system is capable of expressing. Its outputs are inert data — rows in configuration stores — that operational processes read.

Configuration processes do not read operational stores and do not invoke operational processes. They write only to configuration stores, with a single explicit exception: **8.0 Manage Events and Programs is the sole configuration process permitted to create an operational record**, and only because a Program Item has been explicitly linked to a Service Definition.

---

## The processes

| # | Process | Responsibility |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1.0 | Configure Vocabulary | Roles, Duties, Branches, Time Slots, Service Definitions, Service Definition Duties, Service Schedules, and D10 lookups |
| 2.0 | Configure Duty Rules | Duty Rule rows expressing tiered criteria for ranking duty candidates |
| 3.0 | Manage Membership | Member records and Identifier History |
| 4.0 | Manage Eligibility | Eligibility grant/revoke records |
| 8.0 | Manage Events and Programs | Events, Programs, Program Items, Event Duties; event-sourced occurrences when a Program Item links to a Service Definition |

---

## The layer boundary

**Reads:** configuration stores only.

**Writes:** configuration stores only, plus the single 8.0 exception above.

**Does not touch:** `D7 Roster Assignment`, `D8 Attendance Record`, `D12 Materializer Run`, and any operational process.

**Does not invoke:** 5.0, 6.0, 7.0, 9.0, 10.0, or 11.0.

---

## Universal lifecycle semantics

Configuration entities whose contracts define lifecycle state use immediate activation and deactivation rather than deletion. Lifecycle semantics are defined per entity in the sections that follow. Entities whose contracts do not define lifecycle state — `IdentifierHistory`, `Eligibility`, `SystemSetting`, and `D10` lookup values — are governed by their own specific rules.

For entities that do carry lifecycle state, the rules are uniform:

- **Creation is immediate.** An entity is active from the moment it is created. No draft, no publish step, no approval gate.
- **Deactivation removes the entity from future use** without altering any record that references it.
- **Every field is editable, always.** Edits take effect for future use. No backward propagation to operational records.
- **No deletion, ever.** Configuration entities are historical facts about what the organization configured at a given time.

Entities with lifecycle state: `Role`, `Duty`, `Branch`, `BranchTimeSlot`, `ServiceDefinition`, `ServiceDefinitionDuty`, `ServiceSchedule`, `Member`, `DutyRule`, `Event`, `Program`, `ProgramItem`, `EventDuty`.

Entities without lifecycle state: `IdentifierHistory`, `Eligibility`, `SystemSetting`, `Admin`, `D10` lookup values.

---

## Universal invariants

These hold across all five configuration processes:

1. **Rule neutrality.** No configuration process creates a link between `Role` and `Duty` except through a `DutyRule` row written by 2.0. No configuration process pre-associates any entity with any other.
2. **Deactivation, not deletion.** Configuration entities are never removed.
3. **No backward propagation.** Editing any configuration row affects future use only. No operational record is ever rewritten by a configuration change.
4. **Referential integrity.** Every foreign key references an existing row.
5. **No evaluation.** No configuration process evaluates any rule, threshold, or criterion.
6. **No orchestration.** No configuration process invokes any other process.
7. **Authorization upstream.** Configuration processes assume the caller has passed the applicable `PermissionTier` check.
8. **Immediate activation.** Created entities are active from creation, where the entity has lifecycle state.

---

## The 8.0 exception

8.0 Manage Events and Programs is the only configuration process that writes to an operational store. When a Program Item is linked to a Service Definition — at creation or by later edit — the system creates an event-sourced `ServiceOccurrence` row with:

- `EventID` populated
- `ScheduleID` null
- `GeneratedBy = Administrator`
- `CreatedBy` = the admin who created the Program Item

This is a deliberate exception. Event-sourced occurrences bypass the Occurrence Materializer entirely; their lifecycle is governed by the Event/Program process, not by 11.0.

---

## Reading order

For a reader new to the configuration layer:

1. **This README** — the layer overview.
2. **`1.0-configure-vocabulary.md`** — the process that establishes the vocabulary.
3. **`2.0-configure-duty-rules.md`** — the process that expresses how duties rank candidates.
4. **`3.0-manage-membership.md`** — the process that maintains the member register.
5. **`4.0-manage-eligibility.md`** — the process that grants and revokes candidacy.
6. **`8.0-manage-events-and-programs.md`** — the process that manages the event lifecycle.

---

*Source: System Design Specification §9.*

# 10.0 Evaluate Fill Status

*Computes each occurrence's fill state and writes it to FillStatusID. Derived from the System Design Specification §12. Where this document conflicts with the specification, the specification wins.*

---

## Responsibility

10.0 Evaluate Fill Status computes each occurrence's fill state and writes it to ServiceOccurrence.FillStatusID. It reads the occurrence's assignments, the occurrence's required duty list, and the mapping settings that connect mechanical conditions to administrator-defined vocabulary.

It does not create assignments, does not modify assignments, does not modify any occurrence field other than FillStatusID, and does not invoke any other process.

**Source:** System Design Specification §12.

---

## Trigger

Two triggers, within the same process:

- **After assignment changes.** When 5.0 writes assignments, when 7.0 updates statuses, when 7.0 creates replacements.
- **Scheduled sweep.** Periodically, to catch occurrences whose state changed without a direct trigger.

**Source:** System Design Specification §12.0.

---

## The footprint

| Direction | Store |
| --- | --- |
| Reads | D3 (ServiceOccurrence, ServiceDefinitionDuty, ServiceOccurrenceDuty), D7 (RosterAssignment — active assignments per duty), D10 (OutcomeState), D11 (SystemSetting — OutcomeStateUnfilledID, OutcomeStatePartiallyFilledID, OutcomeStateFilledID, OutcomeStateCancelledID optional) |
| Writes | D3 (ServiceOccurrence.FillStatusID only) |

**Never reads or writes:** D1, D2, D4, D5, D6, D8, D9, D12. Never modifies RosterAssignment. Never modifies ServiceOccurrenceDuty. Never invokes any process.

**Source:** System Design Specification §12.2.

---

## The mechanical classification

For each occurrence, 10.0 computes the count of **active** assignments (non-null, non-terminal AssignmentStatusID) across all required duties, and compares it against the **effective required count** (the sum of RequiredSlotCount for each duty, after applying ServiceOccurrenceDuty overrides).

Three mechanical conditions result:

| Condition | Mechanical meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Unfilled | Zero active assignments across all required duties |
| Partially Filled | Active assignments exist, but fewer than the effective required count |
| Filled | Active assignments meet or exceed the effective required count |

**Active** means: AssignmentStatusID is non-null and the referenced status has `IsTerminal = false`.

**Effective required count** means: the sum of RequiredSlotCount across all required duties, after applying ServiceOccurrenceDuty overrides (`Added` adds a duty; `Removed` removes it; `RequiredSlotCount override` replaces the inherited count).

**Source:** System Design Specification §12.1.2.

---

## Zero-duty occurrences

If the effective required count is zero — no required duties for the occurrence, or all duties removed via ServiceOccurrenceDuty overrides — the occurrence is mechanically classified as **Filled**, because the active assignment count (zero) meets the effective required count (zero).

This is a consequence of the mechanical rule, not a special case. It is consistent with the locked zero-duty rule from the 11.0 contract.

**Admin-facing distinction:** both a filled occurrence (all duties satisfied) and a zero-duty occurrence (no duties required) classify mechanically as Filled. An admin-facing dashboard may label them differently — e.g., "Filled" versus "Filled (no duties required)" — so the two facts are distinguishable at a glance. This is a presentation concern, not a schema or process concern; the mechanical classification remains unchanged.

**Source:** System Design Specification §12.1.2, §12.5.

---

## OutcomeState mapping

Three required settings map the three mechanical conditions to administrator-defined OutcomeState entries:

- `OutcomeStateUnfilledID` — the OutcomeStateID to write when the condition is Unfilled
- `OutcomeStatePartiallyFilledID` — when Partially Filled
- `OutcomeStateFilledID` — when Filled

The mechanical classification is fixed. The vocabulary is administrator-defined. The mapping between them is configuration, expressed via SystemSetting.

If any of the three is unset or invalid, 10.0 refuses to run entirely — it does not partially evaluate occurrences. The run fails with a configuration error, consistent with the required-setting pattern used elsewhere.

**Source:** System Design Specification §12.1.3.

---

## Cancellation exclusion

Cancellation is outside the three mechanical fill classifications. 10.0 does not assign the cancelled state and does not replace an existing cancelled FillStatusID.

The cancelled state is identified via `SystemSetting.OutcomeStateCancelledID`:

- **Required = false.** Cancellation is optional in the operational model.
- Holds an OutcomeStateID.
- If configured, 10.0 skips any occurrence whose current FillStatusID equals that ID.
- If absent, no automatic cancellation exclusion applies and 10.0 evaluates every occurrence.
- 10.0 never writes the cancelled state.

The three mechanical mapping settings remain independently required. The cancelled-state setting is the fourth, and is the only one that is not required.

**Source:** System Design Specification §12.1.4.

---

## Single field written

10.0 writes only ServiceOccurrence.FillStatusID. No other column of ServiceOccurrence is touched. No other store is written.

**Source:** System Design Specification §12.1.1.

---

## Idempotency

10.0 is idempotent. Running it multiple times on the same occurrence produces the same FillStatusID as long as the underlying assignments haven't changed. Once FillStatusID is written, re-running 10.0 with unchanged data writes the same value.

**Source:** System Design Specification §12.1.5.

---

## No downstream trigger

10.0 does not invoke any process. The FillStatusID it writes is read by administrator dashboards and by any future process that consumes it. No notification is triggered by fill status changes.

**Source:** System Design Specification §12.1.6.

---

## Invariants

- Mechanical classification: Unfilled, Partially Filled, or Filled, based on active assignment counts and effective required counts.
- Active means non-null, non-terminal AssignmentStatusID.
- Effective required count includes ServiceOccurrenceDuty overrides.
- Zero-required-count occurrence is Filled.
- Required-setting failure: any of the three mechanical mapping settings unset or invalid → 10.0 refuses to run entirely. No partial evaluation.
- Cancellation exclusion is optional. If OutcomeStateCancelledID is configured, 10.0 skips occurrences whose FillStatusID equals that ID. If absent, no exclusion applies.
- 10.0 never writes cancellation.
- Idempotent.
- Single field written: ServiceOccurrence.FillStatusID.
- No downstream trigger.
- No notification.
- No assignment modification.
- No occurrence field other than FillStatusID.

**Source:** System Design Specification §12.3.

---

## Candidate access patterns

- **`RosterAssignment (OccurrenceID, DutyID)`.** Serves fill-state computation: "count active assignments for this occurrence and duty." The existing index from §10 covers this.
- **`ServiceOccurrence (FillStatusID)`.** Serves "which occurrences are unfilled" queries from administrator dashboards. Whether warranted depends on whether such queries are common.

Physical forms are implementation-dependent.

**Source:** System Design Specification §12.4.

---

## Cross-references

- **Specification §7** — the materialization contract; 11.0 leaves FillStatusID null.
- **Specification §8** — the derivation of the occurrence creation contract.
- **Specification §12** — the full derivation.
- **`5.0-generate-assignment.md`** — produces the assignments 10.0 counts.
- **`7.0-manage-confirmation.md`** — transitions the statuses that determine terminality.
- **`../database/settings.md`** — the four OutcomeState mapping settings.

---

*Source: System Design Specification §12.*

# 11.0 Materialize Occurrences

*Generates Service Occurrence records from active Service Schedules on a rolling time horizon. Additive and idempotent. Derived from the System Design Specification §7 and §8. Where this document conflicts with the specification, the specification wins.*

---

## Responsibility

11.0 Materialize Occurrences is a system-internal, time-triggered process that ensures Service Occurrence rows exist for every active Service Schedule up to a configured horizon, expressed in days.

It is the process that produces the *what* of the roster — the occurrence exists. Filling it with members is 5.0's responsibility.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7.

---

## Trigger

Two triggers, sharing identical logic:

- **Scheduled run.** Daily, using the application's existing scheduled-job mechanism. The frequency is fixed, not administrator-configurable.
- **Manual run.** An administrator can invoke the process with an explicit horizon in days. A smaller manual horizon is a safe no-op for dates already covered.

Neither trigger invokes the process indirectly. 11.0 is not invoked by any other process.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (Trigger and scheduling).

---

## The central invariant

**The Occurrence Materializer is additive and idempotent. It creates missing schedule-sourced occurrences within the configured horizon and never modifies or deletes an occurrence that already exists.**

This is the load-bearing rule of the entire process. Every other property derives from it.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (The resulting invariant).

---

## The footprint

| Direction | Store |
| --- | --- |
| Reads | D11 (SystemSetting — OccurrenceHorizonDays); D3 (active ServiceSchedules joined to BranchTimeSlot and ServiceDefinition; existing ServiceOccurrences for existence check) |
| Writes | D3 (ServiceOccurrence inserts only); D12 (MaterializerRun) |

**Never reads or writes:** D1, D2, D4, D5, D6, D7, D8, D9, D10. Never modifies any existing occurrence.

**Source:** System Design Specification §8.2.

---

## The lifecycle

```

Active ServiceSchedule
│
▼
Within [today, today + N]
│
▼
Occurrence already exists?
├── YES → do nothing
└── NO  → INSERT

```

Where N is `OccurrenceHorizonDays` from D11.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (Occurrence creation contract).

---

## Horizon configuration

`OccurrenceHorizonDays` is a required System Setting:

- Type: integer, unit days
- Minimum: 0 (0 means materialize today only)
- Global, not per-branch or per-schedule
- No system-assumed default

If unconfigured, the Materializer refuses to run and surfaces a configuration error. It does not idle silently. A change to the horizon takes effect on the next run; changing the setting is not itself a trigger.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (Horizon configuration).

---

## Schedule eligibility

A schedule is eligible for materialization when the schedule itself is active, its Service Definition is active, and its Branch Time Slot is active. All three must be active for the schedule to produce occurrences.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (Schedule lifecycle interaction).

---

## Occurrence creation contract

For each active schedule:

1. Generate candidate dates from today through today + horizon, inclusive, using the configured system/application timezone.
2. For each date, check whether a Service Occurrence already exists for that (ScheduleID, Date) pair.
3. If one exists, do nothing.
4. If not, insert one with:
   - `ScheduleID` = the schedule's ID
   - `Date` = the missing date
   - `ServiceTypeID` = inherited from the schedule's Service Definition
   - `StartTime` = inherited from the schedule
   - `FillStatusID` = null
   - `GeneratedBy` = System
   - `CreatedBy` = null
   - `ChangedBy` = null

Each occurrence commits in its own transaction. The process is fully re-entrant — an interrupted run simply leaves remaining dates for the next run to fill.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (Occurrence creation contract).

---

## Idempotency

The process is idempotent because of one rule: a date that already has a ServiceOccurrence row — system-generated or carrying an administrator override — is never touched again on a later run.

This is what guarantees that an override made today for a service three weeks out can never be silently overwritten by a subsequent run. The override survives.

The uniqueness constraint on (ScheduleID, Date) for schedule-sourced rows is the correctness guard at the database level. The application-level existence check is a pre-write optimization.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (Immutability), §8.1.3.

---

## Immutability

An existing occurrence is never updated or deleted by 11.0. No exception. Cancellation is represented through the Outcome State, not row deletion. No tombstone mechanism exists.

`GeneratedBy` is permanent provenance, set once at creation and never altered. `ChangedBy` separately records any later human modification.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (Immutability).

---

## Time handling

- `Date` is date-only. Start time is represented separately on the occurrence and is unaffected by this process.
- The process uses the configured system/application timezone. Not a per-branch timezone; "today" must have one deterministic calendar meaning.
- No daylight-saving-specific logic exists in this process. The existing schedule start time is treated as a plain wall-clock value.
- No holiday or blackout exclusions are built in.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (Time handling).

---

## Schedule lifecycle interaction

- **Newly created schedule:** picked up automatically on the next run.
- **Schedule change:** occurrences already materialized from it are never rewritten.
- **Schedule deactivation:** existing occurrences remain; the schedule stops being eligible for future generation. Deactivation, not deletion, is the mechanism for stopping generation.
- **Service Definition duty composition change:** never propagated backward into already-materialized occurrences.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (Schedule lifecycle interaction).

---

## Zero-duty occurrences

An occurrence is created even if its Service Definition currently has zero configured duties. The existence of a scheduled service is independent of whether duties have been configured for it.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (Occurrence creation contract).

---

## Service Occurrence Duty

11.0 does not create Service Occurrence Duty rows. Those represent per-occurrence overrides created by an administrator. An occurrence with no override simply inherits its Service Definition's duty list.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (Occurrence creation contract).

---

## Operational visibility

Each run produces one Materializer Run record in D12:

- TriggerType (Scheduled or Manual)
- TriggeredBy (Admin, populated only when Manual)
- StartedAt, CompletedAt
- SchedulesEvaluated, OccurrencesCreated
- Status (Success or Failure)
- ErrorDetail (populated on failure)

A failed run is recoverable simply by running again. No separate retry or incident subsystem exists. No dry-run mode and no un-materialize operation exist.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (Operational visibility), §8.1.5.

---

## Event-sourced occurrences

Occurrences created when an administrator links a Program Item to a Service Definition are entirely outside this process. They carry `GeneratedBy = Administrator` and `CreatedBy` = the admin who created them. Their uniqueness is governed by the Event/Program process, not by the (ScheduleID, Date) rule.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (Event-sourced occurrences).

---

## Explicit non-relationships

- Independent of Assignment generation. Never invokes 5.0.
- Never sends a notification. Never invokes 9.0.
- Never touches 10.0, 6.0, or 7.0.
- No readiness flag is produced; 5.0 queries occurrences directly.
- OwningBranch enforcement is not this process's concern. It consumes already-valid schedules.

**Source:** System Design Specification §7 (Explicit non-relationships).

---

## Invariants

- **Additive.** No UPDATE or DELETE against ServiceOccurrence.
- **Idempotent.** Repeated runs produce no new rows once coverage is complete.
- **Horizon boundedness.** No row created with Date outside [today, today + N].
- **Schedule-source attribution.** Every created row has a non-null ScheduleID, GeneratedBy = System, CreatedBy = null, ChangedBy = null, FillStatusID = null.
- **Provenance immutability.** GeneratedBy never changes.
- **Schedule deactivation preserves existing rows.** No cascade.
- **Schedule change does not rewrite existing occurrences.**
- **Service Definition change does not propagate backward.**
- **Full re-entrancy.** No checkpoint state.
- **Manual and scheduled runs produce identical results** for the same horizon and time.
- **Required-setting failure.** Missing OccurrenceHorizonDays → zero rows written, one Materializer Run with Status = Failure.
- **Uniqueness.** No two schedule-sourced rows share (ScheduleID, Date).
- **Event-sourced exclusion.** Never creates, modifies, or deletes a row with a non-null EventID.
- **Zero-duty occurrence creation.** Even with no duties, the occurrence exists.
- **Timezone determinism.** "Today" resolves to a single calendar date.
- **No outbound flow.** No notification, no assignment generation, no writes outside D3 and D12.

**Source:** System Design Specification §8.3.

---

## Candidate access patterns

- **`ServiceOccurrence (ScheduleID, Date)` — UNIQUE, restricted to schedule-sourced rows.** The correctness guard and the backing index for the existence check.
- **`ServiceSchedule (IsActive) where IsActive = true`.** Serves the query for active schedules.

Both are candidate indexes; the physical form is implementation-dependent.

**Source:** System Design Specification §8.4.

---

## Cross-references

- **Specification §7** — the materialization contract.
- **Specification §8** — the derived consequences.
- **`5.0-generate-assignment.md`** — consumes the occurrences 11.0 produces.
- **`../database/constraints.md`** — the (ScheduleID, Date) uniqueness constraint.
- **`../database/settings.md`** — the OccurrenceHorizonDays setting.

---

*Source: System Design Specification §7, §8.*

# 12.0 Create Manual Assignment

*Directly creates a Roster Assignment from an administrator's selection, bypassing 5.0's tiered candidate ranking while respecting Eligibility Flag criteria and the uniqueness constraint. Derived from the System Design Specification §17. Where this document conflicts with the specification, the specification wins.*

---

## Responsibility

12.0 Create Manual Assignment is the process by which an administrator directly creates a Roster Assignment. It is the operation referenced throughout the specification by `AssignmentSource = Manual` and `AssignedBy`.

It is the only process other than 5.0 that writes a new Roster Assignment row from scratch.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.

---

## Trigger

Administrator action. No scheduled component.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.1 (A1).

---

## The footprint

| Direction | Store |
| --- | --- |
| Reads | D4 (Member — the selected member), D3 (ServiceOccurrence — the target occurrence), D2 (DutyRule — Eligibility Flag tiers only), D6 (Eligibility — only when a DutyRule references Eligibility Flag), D7 (RosterAssignment — uniqueness check) |
| Writes | D7 (RosterAssignment insert only) |
| Invokes | 9.0 Dispatch Notification, only when the created row's AssignmentStatusID is NULL |

**Never reads or writes:** D1, D5, D9, D11, D12. Never modifies ServiceOccurrence. Never modifies ServiceOccurrenceDuty. Never modifies an existing RosterAssignment row.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.4.

---

## Scope

Applies to any Service Occurrence — schedule-sourced or event-sourced. There is no restriction on which occurrences may receive a manual assignment.

No ordering dependency on 5.0. A manual assignment can be created whether or not 5.0 has already run against the occurrence.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.1 (A2, A3).

---

## What is bypassed and what is not

### Bypassed

Manual assignment bypasses Duty Rule tiers whose CriteriaType is:

- Role
- Gender
- Age Range
- Tenure
- Membership Stage
- Branch-Attendance Recency

The administrator may select any member for any duty, including a member that would produce zero candidates under 5.0's automatic resolution. These criteria types express ranking and filtering preferences for the automatic engine; an admin exercising direct judgment is the decision-maker the engine's ranking exists to assist, not replace.

### Not bypassed

Manual assignment does **not** bypass a Duty Rule tier whose CriteriaType is **Eligibility Flag**.

If any configured tier for the duty references an Eligibility Flag, the selected member must hold a current, valid Eligibility grant for that duty — regardless of assignment source.

Eligibility Flag is the one place a locked distinction from earlier in the specification must be protected. It represents a separate, accountable determination made by a specific authority, built with its own grant/revoke audit trail specifically so a sensitive determination would never be reduced to a bypassable preference. Letting manual assignment route around it would quietly undo that protection. Everywhere else, admin discretion is real discretion; here, it is not — because the discretion being protected belongs to whoever grants the Eligibility, not to whoever is creating the roster assignment.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.1 (B1, B2).

---

## What is not enforced

- **Slot capacity.** Manual assignment does not enforce the effective required slot count (RequiredSlotCount, as adjusted by Service Occurrence Duty overrides). An admin may create a manual assignment even when the duty is already at or above its configured count.
- **Per-occurrence availability.** Manual assignment does not enforce 5.0's rule that a member may not hold a non-terminal assignment on the occurrence at all. An admin may knowingly assign the same member to a second duty on the same occurrence.

Both are the double-duty / shortage-exception cases that the specification has deliberately established as admin decision points, not automatic ones.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.1 (B3, B4).

---

## What is enforced

**Uniqueness.** The (MemberID, DutyID, OccurrenceID) uniqueness constraint applies. A manual assignment duplicating an existing combination is rejected.

This is a data-integrity rule, not a business rule. It protects against a genuine error state (the same person recorded twice for the same slot), not a judgment call — nothing about admin discretion should touch it.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.1 (B5).

---

## Eligibility Flag resolution

For each Duty Rule row attached to the target duty where CriteriaType = Eligibility Flag, 12.0 resolves the applicable Eligibility row using the same rule 5.0 uses (§10.1.4): the row with the latest GrantedDate whose RevokedDate is null, tiebroken by the greatest EligibilityID.

If no such row exists, or the resolved row's RevokedDate is set, the manual assignment is rejected.

If the duty has no Duty Rule row with CriteriaType = Eligibility Flag, there is nothing to check, and 12.0 proceeds without consulting Eligibility at all.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.3.2.

---

## Status and lifecycle

### Admin-selected status

The administrator explicitly sets `AssignmentStatusID` at creation, to either a specific admin-defined status or `NULL`.

- An admin assigning someone who has already verbally agreed can mark the assignment **confirmed** immediately.
- An admin who still wants the normal response flow leaves it **NULL** and lets 7.0 take over, identically to an automatic assignment.

### Notification behavior

9.0 Dispatch Notification is invoked **only when the created assignment's `AssignmentStatusID` is NULL** at creation. A notification asking someone to confirm something the admin already recorded as confirmed would be actively confusing.

This mirrors exactly how 5.0 and 7.0 already invoke 9.0 — only when a response is genuinely being requested.

### Downstream lifecycle

Once created with `AssignmentStatusID = NULL`, a manual assignment is indistinguishable from an automatic one to every downstream process. 7.0 owns its lifecycle exactly as it would for a 5.0-created row.

`AssignmentSource = Manual` and `AssignedBy` remain as **permanent provenance**, but do not alter 7.0's or 10.0's behavior in any way. Provenance is a historical fact worth keeping; it is not a second lifecycle system.

A manual assignment created with a non-null `AssignmentStatusID` is never picked up by 7.0's timeout sweep. Timeout is measured from `CreatedAt` for assignments awaiting a response; an assignment that was never awaiting one has nothing to time out.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.1 (C1–C4).

---

## Replacement interaction

If a manual assignment later enters 7.0's lifecycle (because it started NULL) and is declined or times out, 7.0's existing re-resolution logic applies without modification. The replacement it creates is `AssignmentSource = Automatic` — the same as any other 7.0-created replacement.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.1 (D1).

---

## What is not added

- No separate audit or history table. The row's own fields — `AssignmentSource`, `AssignedBy`, `CreatedAt` — are the complete audit trail.
- No additional idempotency mechanism. A duplicate submission is rejected by the existing unique constraint.
- No new invocation edge. Creating a manual assignment is an assignment change like any other; 10.0 picks it up via its existing "after assignment changes" trigger.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.1 (F1, G1, H1).

---

## Authorization

This process assumes the caller has already passed the applicable `PermissionTier` check. It does not itself define who is allowed to create a manual assignment.

This is identical to every configuration process's stated boundary — authorization is enforced upstream, consistently, everywhere in this specification.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.1 (E1).

---

## Invariants

- Duty Rule's Role, Gender, Age Range, Tenure, Membership Stage, and Branch-Attendance Recency criteria do not gate manual assignment.
- Duty Rule's Eligibility Flag criterion does gate manual assignment, identically to how it gates automatic assignment.
- Slot capacity is not enforced.
- Per-occurrence availability is not enforced.
- (MemberID, DutyID, OccurrenceID) uniqueness is enforced, without exception.
- AssignmentStatusID is administrator-selected, not forced to NULL.
- 9.0 is invoked only when AssignmentStatusID is NULL at creation.
- Once in 7.0's lifecycle, a manually-created assignment is governed identically to an automatically-created one.
- AssignmentSource = Manual and AssignedBy are permanent provenance.
- 12.0 never modifies an existing row.
- 12.0 never writes to Service Occurrence.
- No new audit table is introduced.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.5.

---

## Indexes

No new index is required. The uniqueness check is served by the existing `RosterAssignment (MemberID, DutyID, OccurrenceID)` unique constraint. The Eligibility lookup is served by the existing `Eligibility (MemberID, DutyID)` composite index.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.6.

---

## Explicitly open implementation choices

- Whether the admin-facing interface warns when a selection would not satisfy Duty Rule's non-Eligibility criteria, versus allowing it silently. The contract permits the bypass either way.
- Whether a confirmation step exists between an admin's selection and the write (e.g., a review screen).
- Editing or removing an existing assignment — explicitly out of scope.

**Source:** System Design Specification §17.7.

---

## Cross-references

- **Specification §10** — Generate Assignment, whose Eligibility-resolution rule this process reuses exactly.
- **Specification §11** — Manage Confirmation, which governs a manually-created assignment identically to an automatic one.
- **Specification §14** — Dispatch Notification, invoked under the same condition 5.0 and 7.0 already use.
- **Specification §15** — the existing constraints this process relies on.
- **Specification §16** — the invocation model; this process adds no new invocation edge.
- **Specification §17** — the full derivation.

---

*Source: System Design Specification §17.*

# 5.0 Generate Assignment

*Fills the required duty slots of a Service Occurrence by creating Roster Assignment records. Derived from the System Design Specification §10. Where this document conflicts with the specification, the specification wins.*

---

## Responsibility

5.0 Generate Assignment fills the required duty slots on a Service Occurrence by producing Roster Assignment rows. It reads Eligibility to determine candidacy, reads Duty Rule to rank candidates, and writes assignments.

It is the process where the configuration language actually does something. Every Duty Rule, Eligibility grant, and Service Definition Duty is executed here.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.

---

## Trigger

Two triggers, sharing identical logic:

- **Scheduled run.** A recurring run that picks up any occurrence not yet filled, or any occurrence whose assignments have become incomplete.
- **Manual administrator action.** An administrator can invoke the process for a specific occurrence or date range.

5.0 is never invoked by 11.0. The two processes are independent; scheduling may run them close together so newly materialized occurrences are filled promptly, but neither calls the other.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.0, §10.5.

---

## The footprint

| Direction | Store |
| --- | --- |
| Reads | D2 (DutyRule), D3 (ServiceOccurrence, ServiceDefinitionDuty, ServiceOccurrenceDuty, BranchTimeSlot), D4 (Member), D6 (Eligibility), D7 (RosterAssignment — capacity count and availability check), D8 (AttendanceRecord — only when a Branch-Attendance Recency criterion is present) |
| Writes | D7 (RosterAssignment inserts only) |
| Invokes | 9.0 Dispatch Notification, on each new automatic assignment |

**Never reads or writes:** D1, D5, D9, D10, D11, D12. Never modifies ServiceOccurrence. Never modifies existing RosterAssignment rows.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.2.

---

## The two-mechanism model

5.0 applies two distinct mechanisms, and they are never allowed to collapse into each other:

**Eligibility gates candidacy.** A member is a candidate for a duty if and only if they are eligible for that duty. Eligibility is a binary, administrator-granted permission record.

**Duty Rule ranks candidates.** Duty Rule orders eligible candidates by tier. It does not determine who is a candidate; it determines the order of preference among those who already are.

The two are independent. A member can be eligible and fail every Duty Rule, or ineligible and match every Duty Rule — in which case they never become a candidate at all.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1.4, §10.1.5.

---

## Eligibility resolution

For each (Member, Duty) pair, 5.0 determines eligibility by resolving the applicable Eligibility row:

1. The row whose GrantedDate is in the past.
2. Whose RevokedDate is either null or in the future.
3. If multiple such rows exist, the one with the greatest EligibilityID is used as the deterministic tiebreaker.

If the resolved row has a RevokedDate in the past, the member is not eligible. If no row exists at all, the member is not eligible. Eligibility is opt-in.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1.4.

---

## Duty Rule evaluation

Tiers are evaluated in ascending TierOrder. For each tier:

1. All rules sharing that TierOrder are combined with AND.
2. A member matches the tier if every rule at that tier evaluates true for them.
3. The first tier producing at least one eligible, available candidate supplies the candidate set.

If no Duty Rules exist for a duty, every eligible member enters as an equal candidate.

If a tier consists entirely of rules whose criteria types are reserved (Acceptance Rate, Duties Carried, Days Since Last Assignment), the tier is treated as producing no candidates.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1.5, §5.

---

## Criteria evaluation

| CriteriaType | Evaluation |
| --- | --- |
| Role | Member's RoleID matches the rule's CriteriaValue (resolved by name) |
| Gender | Member's Gender matches |
| Age Range | Computed from DateOfBirth and today's date, within the range |
| Tenure | Computed from JoinDate and today's date, meets the threshold |
| Membership Stage | Member's MembershipStage matches |
| Branch-Attendance Recency | Member has an AttendanceRecord at the specified branch within the configured window |
| Eligibility Flag | Member has a current Eligibility grant for the referenced duty |

Each criterion is a mechanical evaluation against the member's stored data. The system does not interpret the criterion beyond what its type defines.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1.5.

---

## Availability

A member is available for a duty on an occurrence if and only if they do not have a **non-terminal** Roster Assignment on that OccurrenceID.

Terminal assignments — those whose AssignmentStatusID references a status with `IsTerminal = true` — do not block availability. They represent slots the member no longer occupies.

The availability rule uses `AssignmentStatus.IsTerminal`, the same boolean used by 7.0's re-resolution logic and 10.0's capacity calculation.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1.2.

---

## Capacity — the slot-count rule

For each (OccurrenceID, DutyID) pair, the effective slot count is determined by:

1. The RequiredSlotCount from ServiceDefinitionDuty for the duty.
2. Overridden by ServiceOccurrenceDuty.RequiredSlotCount if an override row exists with Action = Added.
3. If a ServiceOccurrenceDuty row exists with Action = Removed, the duty is not required for that occurrence, and no slots are filled.

5.0 counts existing non-terminal assignments for the pair and fills up to the remaining count. Terminal assignments do not count toward the total.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1.3.

---

## Selection within a tier

When a tier produces more candidates than slots, 5.0 fills from the candidate set in a deterministic order. The order is defined by 5.0 (candidate ordering by ascending MemberID is a defensible concrete choice).

No fairness guarantee is made. No rotation, load-balancing, or recency-based ordering is applied. Reserved fairness criteria may activate later, at which point a tier using them produces a meaningfully ordered candidate set.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1.6.

---

## One member, one occurrence

5.0 does not assign a member to more than one duty on the same occurrence. If a member is already assigned to any duty on an occurrence (with a non-terminal status), they are excluded from candidacy for any further duty on that occurrence.

This is the availability rule from B7. The distinction is worth preserving: it is not "one member, one duty" in the abstract — it is "one member, one assignment on this occurrence."

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1.2.

---

## No fallback

If no tier produces a candidate, the duty's slots remain unfilled. No fallback to lower tiers. No notification of non-assignment.

If fewer candidates exist than slots, the remaining slots remain unfilled. No top-up from lower tiers.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1.5.

---

## Additivity

5.0 never removes or modifies an existing Roster Assignment. It is additive. If a duty already has some assignments, 5.0 fills only the remaining slots.

Removal or replacement is a 7.0 concern (on decline or timeout) or an explicit administrator action.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1.7.

---

## Initial status

5.0 writes assignments with `AssignmentStatusID = NULL`. The assignment lifecycle begins with 7.0; 5.0 does not claim a lifecycle state.

It also writes:

- `AssignmentSource = Automatic`
- `AssignedBy = NULL`
- `ApprovedBy = NULL`
- `CreatedAt` = the moment of insertion

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1.1.

---

## Uniqueness

Roster Assignment is unique on (MemberID, DutyID, OccurrenceID). This is the database-level guard against duplicate records, regardless of source.

The capacity rule (counting assignments per (OccurrenceID, DutyID) up to RequiredSlotCount) governs how many slots are filled. The uniqueness rule governs what combinations may coexist. Both are required.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1.7.

---

## Re-entry

5.0 is fully re-entrant. An interrupted run resumes on the next run; existing assignments are counted, remaining slots are filled. No checkpoint state.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1.7.

---

## Interaction with 9.0

When 5.0 creates a new automatic assignment, it invokes 9.0 Dispatch Notification with that assignment as input. 9.0 sends an assignment notice to the member.

The invocation is fire-and-forget. 5.0 does not wait for the notification result; 9.0's failure does not roll back the assignment.

No other process is invoked by 5.0.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1.8.

---

## Manual assignments

An administrator can create a Roster Assignment manually, bypassing 5.0's candidate selection. The row carries `AssignmentSource = Manual` and `AssignedBy = <AdminID>`.

A manual assignment can violate Eligibility — the administrator is assumed authorized to override. The (MemberID, DutyID, OccurrenceID) uniqueness constraint applies regardless of source.

The MKN Configuration Reference sets a default that manual assignments are excluded from future fairness signals. This is a policy, not a system rule.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.1, MKN Configuration Reference.

---

## Invariants

- Eligibility gates candidacy; Duty Rule only ranks.
- Tiers are evaluated in ascending TierOrder.
- Same-tier rules are ANDed.
- No tier → every eligible member is an equal candidate.
- No candidate in any tier → slot remains unfilled, no fallback.
- Fewer candidates than slots → remaining slots unfilled, no top-up.
- 5.0 never removes or modifies an existing assignment.
- 5.0 does not assign a member to more than one duty on the same occurrence.
- (MemberID, DutyID, OccurrenceID) is unique in RosterAssignment.
- 5.0 is fully re-entrant.
- AssignmentStatusID is null at creation.
- AssignmentSource = Automatic, AssignedBy = null for automatic assignments.
- 5.0 never writes FillStatusID.
- 5.0 never modifies ServiceOccurrence.
- 5.0 invokes only 9.0.
- 5.0 is independent of 11.0.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.3.

---

## Candidate access patterns

- **`RosterAssignment (MemberID, DutyID, OccurrenceID)` — UNIQUE.** The integrity guard.
- **`RosterAssignment (OccurrenceID, DutyID)`.** Serves the capacity count and availability check. Hot path for filling slots.
- **`Eligibility (MemberID, DutyID)`.** Serves eligibility resolution.
- **`DutyRule (DutyID, TierOrder)`.** Serves tier evaluation.

Physical forms are implementation-dependent.

**Source:** System Design Specification §10.4.

---

## Cross-references

- **Specification §5** — the configuration language 5.0 evaluates.
- **Specification §10** — the full derivation.
- **`11.0-materialize-occurrences.md`** — produces the occurrences 5.0 fills.
- **`7.0-manage-confirmation.md`** — owns the assignment lifecycle from where 5.0 leaves off.
- **`9.0-dispatch-notification.md`** — invoked by 5.0 on each new assignment.
- **`10.0-evaluate-fill-status.md`** — reads the assignments 5.0 produces.
- **`../database/constraints.md`** — the (MemberID, DutyID, OccurrenceID) uniqueness constraint.

---

*Source: System Design Specification §5, §10.*

LINES: 160

# 6.0 Record Attendance

*Records the raw fact of a member's presence at an occurrence. Derived from the System Design Specification §13. Where this document conflicts with the specification, the specification wins.*

---

## Responsibility

6.0 Record Attendance writes AttendanceRecord rows when a member's presence at an occurrence is registered. It is the narrowest process in the system — it reads two stores, writes one, and has no downstream consequences within the system's own logic.

Attendance is a raw fact. 6.0 does not interpret it, does not condition it on assignment, does not affect fill status, does not affect assignment status, and does not trigger any other process.

**Source:** System Design Specification §13.

---

## Trigger

Two triggers:

- **Member action at an occurrence.** An NFC tap or other configured channel, registered at the time of the occurrence.
- **Manual administrator entry.** An administrator can add an attendance record directly.

**Source:** System Design Specification §13.0.

---

## The footprint

| Direction | Store |
| --- | --- |
| Reads | D4 (Member — to validate the member identity), D3 (ServiceOccurrence — to identify which occurrence the record applies to) |
| Writes | D8 (AttendanceRecord — one row per (MemberID, OccurrenceID) pair) |

**Never reads or writes:** D1, D2, D5, D6, D7, D9, D10, D11, D12. Never modifies ServiceOccurrence in any field. Never writes FillStatusID. Never touches any assignment.

**Source:** System Design Specification §13.2.

---

## The AttendanceRecord row

| Column | Value |
| --- | --- |
| RecordID | System-generated |
| MemberID | The member whose presence is recorded |
| OccurrenceID | The occurrence at which presence is recorded |
| Timestamp | The moment the record is written |

No other columns. The row is the raw fact.

**Source:** System Design Specification §13.1.1.

---

## Uniqueness

`AttendanceRecord` permits at most one recorded attendance fact for a given (MemberID, OccurrenceID) pair. The uniqueness constraint is the integrity guard.

A subsequent tap for an existing pair is a no-op. It does not create a second row, and the application-level check treats it as a no-op rather than an error.

This makes 6.0 idempotent per (MemberID, OccurrenceID). Re-running the same tap produces no new row.

**Source:** System Design Specification §13.1.2.

---

## Independence from assignment

6.0 does not check whether the member is assigned to the occurrence. A member may be:

- Rostered and attend.
- Rostered and not attend.
- Not rostered and attend.
- Not rostered and not attend.

All four are valid states. Attendance is a fact; assignment is a plan. The system does not conflate them.

**Source:** System Design Specification §13.1.3.

---

## Branch-agnostic attendance

Attendance is recorded against the occurrence, not the member's home branch. A member attending at a branch other than their home branch produces an attendance record against that occurrence, with no reference to Member.BranchID.

This is consistent with the MKN Configuration Reference's branch-agnostic attendance default.

**Source:** System Design Specification §13.1.3.

---

## No attendance-window enforcement

No attendance-window enforcement is performed by 6.0 or the schema. The system does not prevent attendance before, during, or after the occurrence's scheduled window. Any such enforcement, if introduced, is outside the current contract.

**Source:** System Design Specification §13.3.

---

## Manual administrator entry

An administrator may add an attendance record directly — for a member whose tap failed, for a retrospective correction, or for another operational reason. The row is identical to a tap-initiated one: same columns, same uniqueness rule.

The schema has no field distinguishing manual entry from tap entry. If audit is later required, adding such a field is an additive change; it is not in the current contract.

**Source:** System Design Specification §13.1.4.

---

## No interpretation layer

6.0 does not compute attendance recency. It does not update any cache or derived field. The Branch-Attendance Recency criterion used by Duty Rule evaluation (5.0) reads AttendanceRecord at evaluation time, not from any precomputed state.

This keeps 6.0 write-only to D8 and leaves all interpretation to consumers.

**Source:** System Design Specification §13.1.5.

---

## Invariants

- Raw fact. Attendance is a fact of presence, not an interpretation. 6.0 writes it and stops.
- Uniqueness. AttendanceRecord permits at most one recorded attendance fact for a given (MemberID, OccurrenceID) pair.
- Idempotent per member per occurrence. Re-running the same tap produces no new row.
- Independence from assignment. Attendance is not conditioned on RosterAssignment.
- Branch-agnostic. Attendance is recorded against the occurrence, not the member's home branch.
- No attendance-window enforcement.
- No interpretation. 6.0 does not compute recency, streaks, or any derived value.
- No downstream trigger.
- No notification.
- No assignment or fill-status side effect.

**Source:** System Design Specification §13.3.

---

## Candidate access patterns

- **`AttendanceRecord (MemberID, OccurrenceID)` — UNIQUE.** The integrity guard. Also serves the existence check.
- **`AttendanceRecord (OccurrenceID)`.** Natural access pattern for "who attended this occurrence" queries.
- **`AttendanceRecord (MemberID, Timestamp)`.** Natural access pattern for Branch-Attendance Recency evaluation by 5.0 and for member-facing attendance history.

Physical forms are implementation-dependent.

**Source:** System Design Specification §13.4.

---

## Cross-references

- **Specification §13** — the full derivation.
- **`../configuration/3.0-manage-membership.md`** — produces the Member records 6.0 references.
- **`11.0-materialize-occurrences.md`** — produces the occurrences 6.0 records attendance for.
- **`5.0-generate-assignment.md`** — reads AttendanceRecord only when a Branch-Attendance Recency criterion is present.
- **`../database/constraints.md`** — the (MemberID, OccurrenceID) uniqueness constraint.

---

*Source: System Design Specification §13.*
